<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We construct an infinite finitely presented simple amenable group, giving a positive answer to the finite-presentation existence question for simple amenable groups.

- **claim-main-existence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There exists an infinite finitely presented simple amenable group.
  - Explanation: One group is simultaneously infinite, finitely presented, simple and amenable.
- **claim-arithmetic-of-cuts** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-arithmetic)): The following statements hold.* 1. *There is a positive integer $D$, depending only on $\lambda$, such that every intersection of nonparallel allowable lines lies in $D^{-1}\mathcal O^2$. There are finitely many such vertex types modulo $\mathcal O^2$, including the set of allowable directions through the vertex. Each type has representatives arbitrarily close to the origin.* 2. *In each allowable line direction, the group of translations in $\mathcal O^2$ parallel to that line is dense in the line and has a pair of generators of arbitrarily small ordinary length.* 3. *The image of $\mathcal O$ under $z\mapsto(z,\bar z)$ is a lattice in $\mathbb R^2$. There are absolute constants $C,c>0$ such that, for every integer $n\ge1$ and every interval $J$ of $n$ consecutive integers, the points $\{i\tau\bmod1:i\in J\}$ have largest circular gap at most $C/n$; any two distinct points in this set have circular distance at least $c/n$.
  - Explanation: Cut intersections have bounded denominators, tangent translation groups are dense with short generators, and golden-ratio multiples modulo one have uniform gap and separation bounds.
- **claim-strict-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-comparison)): If $U,V\subseteq mX$ are Boolean polygons and $\mu(U)<\mu(V)$, then $U$ has a piecewise translation embedding into $V$.
  - Explanation: A polygon of strictly smaller area embeds into a larger one by a piecewise translation.
- **claim-alternating-extension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-extension)): Let $B,U,V\subseteq mX$ be Boolean polygons with $U,V$ disjoint from $B$. Suppose $f:U\to V$ is a piecewise translation bijection and $$10\mu(U)<\mu(mX\setminus B).$$ There is $a\in A_m$ agreeing with $f$ on $U$ and fixing $B$ pointwise. The assertion includes the empty case $U=\varnothing$.
  - Explanation: A small piecewise translation bijection extends to an alternating element fixing a prescribed region.
- **claim-simplicity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-simplicity)): For every positive integer $m$, the group $A_m$ is infinite, perfect, and simple, and $$[F_m,F_m]=A_m.$$
  - Explanation: The alternating subgroup is infinite, perfect, simple and equals the commutator subgroup of the full group.
- **claim-amenability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-amenability)): For every positive integer $m$, the discrete group $F_m$ is amenable. Every subgroup of $F_m$, in particular $A_m$, satisfies the finite-set Følner condition.
  - Explanation: The full group is amenable and every subgroup satisfies the finite-set Følner condition.
- **claim-am-estimates** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-am-estimates)): For each $g\in\mathcal K$, let $U_g$ be its permutation operator on $\ell^2(\mathcal S)$ and put $h_N=U_g\mu_N-\mu_N$. With $\eta,\kappa$ fixed, $$\begin{aligned} \\|U_gB_NU_g^{-1}-B_N\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}&\longrightarrow0, \label{eq:am-covariance}\\ \Pr\{\\|B_N\xi\\|_\infty>1/2\}&\longrightarrow0, \label{eq:am-concentration} \end{aligned}$$ where the $\xi_z$ are independent, centered random variables supported on $[-1,1]$. Moreover $$\label{eq:am-signal-bound} \limsup_{N\to\infty}\\|(I+B_N)^{-1}h_N\\|_2 \le C(\eta+\sqrt\kappa),$$ where $C$ is independent of sufficiently small $\eta,\kappa$. All assertions hold uniformly over the fixed finite set $\mathcal K$.
  - Explanation: Averaging matrices are nearly covariant, their noise is uniformly small with high probability, and the regularised mean displacement is small.
- **claim-am-density-speed** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-am-density-speed)): Let $\xi\in\mathbb R^d$ have independent coordinates of density $f^2$. Suppose $T_t$ is a differentiable path of invertible real matrices of positive determinant and $\mu_t\in\mathbb R^d$ is differentiable. If $p_t$ is the density of $\mu_t+T_t\xi$, then $$\left\\|\frac{d}{dt}\sqrt{p_t}\right\\|_{L^2} \le C_f\left(\\|T_t^{-1}\dot T_t\\|_{\mathrm{HS}} +\\|T_t^{-1}\dot\mu_t\\|_2\right),$$ with $C_f$ independent of $d$.
  - Explanation: The speed of the square-root density of an affine image of product noise is bounded dimension-free.
- **claim-am-field-covariance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-am-field-covariance)): For any finite list of smooth signals constant outside compact sets, and any finite list of flag types, the laws of the independent fields in <a href="#eq:am-field" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:am-field">[eq:am-field]</a> can be chosen so that, for every $g\in\mathcal K$, the total variation distance between their joint law and its image under $g$ is arbitrarily small on all sites at once. With probability tending to one as $N\to\infty$, every bit is one wherever its signal is at least two, and zero wherever its signal is at most minus two.
  - Explanation: The random bit fields are almost invariant in total variation under each exchange while forcing bits where the signal is large.
- **claim-am-partition-coupling** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-am-partition-coupling)): The signals just chosen have the following property. Given $\delta>0$, one can choose $\eta,\kappa$ and then $N$ so that, for each $g\in\mathcal K$, there is a coupling of two partitions $\mathcal P$ and $\mathcal P'$ having the law of $\mathcal P_N$ for which, with probability greater than $1-\delta$, every cell of $\mathcal P$ is contained in a translation chart of $g$ and $$\mathcal P'=g\mathcal P=\{gC:C\in\mathcal P\}.$$ The equality is as Boolean partitions, including track labels.
  - Explanation: Two samples of the random partition can be coupled so the second is the image of the first with high probability.
- **claim-am-reiter-folner** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-am-reiter-folner)): Let $H$ be a subgroup of a countable group $J$. Suppose that for every finite $K\subset H$ and every $\varepsilon>0$ there is a probability measure $\nu$ on $J$ such that $$\sum_{k\in K}\\|k\nu-\nu\\|_1<\varepsilon.$$ Then for every finite $K\subset H$ and every $\varepsilon>0$ there is a nonempty finite $A\subset H$ such that $$\sum_{k\in K}\|kA\mathbin\triangle A\|<\varepsilon\|A\|.$$
  - Explanation: Almost invariant probability measures yield finite Følner sets inside a subgroup.
- **claim-multiplier-finite-generation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-multiplier)): There is an integer $m_{\mathrm{hom}}$ such that $H_2(A_m;\mathbb Z)$ is finitely generated for every finite $m\geq m_{\mathrm{hom}}$.
  - Explanation: The Schur multiplier of the alternating group is finitely generated for large track number.
- **claim-configuration-acyclic** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-hom-configuration-acyclic)): For each fixed integer $d\geq0$, the augmented complex $C_*(\mathcal E_m)$ has zero homology in degrees $-1,0,\ldots,d$ when $m$ is sufficiently large. The required lower bound on $m$ depends only on $d$.
  - Explanation: The complex of ordered disjoint embedded squares is acyclic in a bounded range for enough tracks.
- **claim-full-group-stability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-hom-full-stability)): For $q=0,1,2$, adding an identity track induces an isomorphism $$H_q(F_{m-1})\longrightarrow H_q(F_m)$$ for all sufficiently large $m$. The same assertion holds with $F_m$ replaced by the finite symmetric group $\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}(m)$.
  - Explanation: Adding an identity track is an isomorphism on low-degree homology of the full and symmetric groups.
- **claim-finiteness-transfers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-hom-finiteness-transfers)): The following statements hold for spaces of CW homotopy type.* 1. *If $Z$ is simply connected, then, for every $r\geq0$, $H_i(Z)$ is finitely generated for $i\leq r+1$ if and only if $H_i(\Omega Z)$ is finitely generated for $i\leq r$.* 2. *Let $Y$ be a connected $H$-space. If $H_i(Y)$ is finitely generated for $i\leq r$, then $\pi_1(Y)$ and $H_i(\widetilde Y)$ for $i\leq r$ are finitely generated, provided $r\geq1$. Conversely, if $\pi_1(Y)$ and those cover homology groups are finitely generated, then $H_i(Y)$ is finitely generated for $i\leq r$.
  - Explanation: Finite generation of homology passes between a space and its loop space or universal cover.
- **claim-cofinal-completion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-hom-cofinal-completion)): There are natural homology isomorphisms $$\begin{aligned} \underset{m}{\operatorname{colim}}\,H_j(F_m) &\cong H_j(\Omega_0 B^1\mathcal B),\\ \underset{m}{\operatorname{colim}}\,H_j(\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}(m)) &\cong H_j(\Omega_0 B^1\mathcal S). \end{aligned}$$ Here $\Omega_0$ denotes the component of the constant loop.
  - Explanation: Stable homology of the track and symmetric groups is the homology of the neutral loop component of a bar construction.
- **claim-finite-set-bars** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-hom-finite-set-bars)): For $d=1,2,3$, the groups $H_i(B^d\mathcal S)$ are finitely generated for $i\leq d+2$.
  - Explanation: Iterated bar constructions on finite sets have finitely generated homology in a range.
- **claim-string-resolution** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-hom-string-resolution)): There is a zigzag of natural realization equivalences between $B^3\mathcal B$ and the realization in $p$ of $B^3\mathcal C_p$.
  - Explanation: The threefold bar construction of the polygon groupoid is equivalent to the realization of threefold bars of string groupoids.
- **claim-corner-module** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-hom-corner-module)): The $R$-module $L$ is finitely generated.
  - Explanation: Polygonal step functions form a finitely generated module over the Laurent ring via corner germs.
- **claim-coefficient-finiteness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-hom-coefficient-finiteness)): For every finitely generated abelian group $K$ and every $i\geq0$, $$H_i\bigl(\Gamma;C(X,\mathbb Z)\otimes K\bigr)$$ is a finitely generated abelian group.
  - Explanation: Translation homology with step-function coefficients tensored with a finitely generated group is finitely generated.
- **claim-full-group-finiteness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-hom-full-finiteness)): For all sufficiently large finite $m$, the groups $H_1(F_m)$ and $H_2(F_m)$ are finitely generated.
  - Explanation: First and second homology of the full group are finitely generated for large finite track number.
- **claim-alternating-surjection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-hom-alternating-surjection)): For all sufficiently large $m$, stabilization induces a surjection $H_2(A_{m-1})\to H_2(A_m)$.
  - Explanation: Stabilisation is onto on second homology of alternating groups for large track number.
- **claim-trivial-conjugation-action** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-hom-trivial-action)): For all sufficiently large finite $m$, conjugation by every element of $F_m$ induces the identity on $H_2(A_m)$.
  - Explanation: The full group acts trivially by conjugation on the second homology of the alternating group.
- **claim-lifting-generation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-lifting-generation)): For sufficiently large finite $m$, the list $S_m$ generates $A_m$. Every polygonal test on a proper alphabet of at least five tracks is generated, in projection to $A_m$, using conditional permutations on translated basic tests on that alphabet and balanced translations.
  - Explanation: The finite list of conditional permutations and balanced translations generates the alternating group.
- **claim-perfect-covers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-perfect-covers)): Every perfect group $E$ has a canonical perfect central extension $E^\ast\twoheadrightarrow E$. It is functorial in $E$. A map from $E$ into the quotient of any central extension has a unique lift from $E^\ast$. Moreover:* 1. *two homomorphisms from a perfect group into a central extension that induce the same quotient map are equal;* 2. *if two perfect subgroups commute modulo a central subgroup, they commute exactly;* 3. *a perfect group $H$ has centerless quotient $H/Z(H)$.
  - Explanation: Every perfect group has a canonical functorial perfect central extension with unique lifting.
- **claim-law-enlargement** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-law-enlargement)): Fix an alphabet $I$ with $\|I\|\ge5$. Suppose a finite test family $\mathcal T_0$ is contained in a finite family $\mathcal T_1$, with literally the same specified input copies on the old tests, including the constant copy. Let $H_0\le K$ be their generated subgroups. Suppose they have central laws for assignment sets $\Omega_0$ and $\Omega_1$, respectively, and restriction of assignments gives a map $r:\Omega_1\to\Omega_0$. Then their canonical representations satisfy $$\rho^{\,0}_{\omega,I}(s) =\prod_{\substack{\omega'\in\Omega_1\\r(\omega')=\omega}} \rho^{\,1}_{\omega',I}(s), \qquad \omega\in\Omega_0,\quad s\in\mathop{\mathrm{Alt}}(I)^\ast.$$ The factors on the right commute. An old assignment with no extension has trivial original representation.
  - Explanation: Old canonical representations are products of the new ones when the test family is enlarged.
- **claim-small-support** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-small-support)): There is an absolute integer $q$ with the following property. Suppose conditional perfect copies are specified coherently on the subalphabets of a finite track set, with a common assignment model $\Omega$. If the central law holds on every alphabet of at most $q$ tracks, then it holds on the entire track set. It suffices to take $q=15$ for this implication. Additional tracks used to prove its hypotheses are a separate fixed reserve. The conclusion concerns the subgroup generated by the indicated test family, not translations or other predicates outside that family.
  - Explanation: A central law verified on small alphabets holds on the whole track set.
- **claim-translated-copies** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-translated-copies)): Let $U$ be a basic test and let $I$ be a proper alphabet. For $u\in\Gamma$, choose $d\in B_m$ with $d_i=u$ for $i\in I$. Then $$\label{eq:translated-copy} \rho_{U+u,I}(s) =t(d)\rho_{U,I}(s)t(d)^{-1}$$ is independent of the balancing values off $I$. The same assertion holds for words and canonical perfect factors obtained from a lawful family on $I$. It has the following consequences.* 1. *Conditional copies on disjoint small alphabets commute exactly, even when their tests have different translation offsets. Products of such translated input copies inherit this property.* 2. *Translating all tests in a fixed finite table by a common $u$ preserves its exact law on a proper alphabet. Thus finite relations for bounded offsets give laws for those offsets relative to any common shift.* 3. *A translation whose coordinates vanish on $I$ commutes with all such aligned words and perfect factors on $I$. Coordinate predicates are unchanged by a shift in the other coordinate.
  - Explanation: Translated primitive copies are independent of balancing off the alphabet and disjoint-alphabet copies commute.
- **claim-control-by-containers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-control)): Assume the relevant small-alphabet laws and the exact disjoint-alphabet commutation of Lemma <a href="#lem:translated-copies" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:translated-copies">28</a>. Assume also that each individual comparison leaves a fresh five-track bank and two parity-correction letters outside the alphabets involved, and one further track for balancing translations.* 1. *If the allowed assignments of a central law imply $P\subseteq J$, they give control inside $J$; disjointness in that assignment model gives commuting perfect representations. In particular, geometric inclusion and disjointness suffice in an actual central law.* 2. *If a fragment is controlled inside $J$ and an aligned word $u$ centralizes the conditional perfect copies on $J$ on the enlarged alphabets used below, then $u$ centralizes that fragment.* 3. *Control is transitive. More generally, if two aligned words have the same conjugation action on the conditional copies on $J$, they have the same action on every fragment controlled inside $J$.* 4. *If $J,K$ have a joint central law and a fragment is controlled inside each, it is controlled inside $J\cap K$. Fragments controlled on disjoint sides of a lawful decomposition commute, even if those fragments do not yet have a joint law with one another.
  - Explanation: Control inside a container is transitive, closed under intersection, and checkable on the container's copies.
- **claim-pair-laws** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-pair-laws)): Suppose a finite collection of partitions has a central law for each individual partition and each pair, coherently on subalphabets, and conditional copies on disjoint alphabets commute exactly. Assume the spare tracks specified in Lemma <a href="#lem:control" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:control">29</a> are available for the individual comparisons. Then the whole collection has the central law for formal assignments, with all exclusions already known in any lawful subfamily retained. Its canonical representations refine all the pairwise decompositions simultaneously.
  - Explanation: Pairwise central laws give a simultaneous central law refining all pairwise decompositions.
- **claim-patching** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-patching)): In the preceding situation, with the spare tracks of Lemma <a href="#lem:control" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:control">29</a> available for each individual comparison, the groups $H_P$ commute for distinct cells, generate a subgroup containing $H$, and are preserved by every original conditional generator. To prove that a word $w$ in those generators is central in $H$, it suffices to prove that it acts trivially on each $H_P$. The following verification is sufficient: for every letter of $w$ choose a replacement whose conjugation action on $H_P$ is the same; require that the replacement word acts trivially on $H_P$.* *Equality of the actions can be established by a chain of container comparisons from Lemma <a href="#lem:control" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:control">29</a>. In particular, a relator central in a lawful template group acts trivially on $H_P$ if the template contains a controlling container $J$ for $H_P$ and its central law is available on the enlarged alphabets needed for the conditional move. This remains true when the individual $U_j$-sides over $P$ are only formal.
  - Explanation: Invariant cell groups commute across cells and centrality of a relator can be checked cell by cell.
- **claim-window-bridge** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-window-bridge)): Assume $\mathcal R(n)$. Fix $A,K,\delta>0$ and an integer $B\ge1$. Suppose a perfect copy is controlled in a rectangle $R=I_x\times I_y$ whose side lengths are at most $A/n$. Each $I_\alpha$ is a union of cells of $\mathcal D(W_\alpha)$, where $$\lfloor n/4\rfloor\le \|W_\alpha\|\le\lfloor0.9n\rfloor,$$ and the source control is available with a label margin at most $B$. Let $J=J_x\times J_y$ be a chart rectangle whose coordinate endpoint labels each lie in an interval of at most $B$ integers. Suppose that these labels are at distance at most $Kn+B$ from a label of the corresponding source window, and that $J$ contains the closed $\delta$-neighborhood of $R$ in the chosen circular coordinate charts. For all sufficiently large $n$, depending only on $A,K,\delta,B$, the copy is controlled in $J$. The proof uses only $\mathcal R(n)$.
  - Explanation: Rectangular control transfers to a distant chart rectangle through a chain of short windows without accumulating error.
- **claim-nested-cuts** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-nested-cuts)): Let $E$ be a perfect group, and suppose $f,l_i,h_i:E\to H$ are homomorphisms satisfying $f(s)=l_i(s)h_i(s)$ and $[l_i(E),h_i(E)]=1$. Order the indices by increasing cuts, and suppose additionally that $[l_i(E),h_j(E)]=1$ whenever $i<j$. Then the successive differences $l_{i-1}(s)^{-1}l_i(s)$ are commuting homomorphisms, with the evident initial and final factors, and give a split representation of the consecutive intervals.
  - Explanation: Ordered cuts with commuting prefix and suffix factors split a homomorphism into commuting interval homomorphisms.
- **claim-rectangular-law** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-rectangular-law)): For a sufficiently large fixed initial window $n_0$, its true table and the finite geometric tables described above imply $\mathcal R(n)$ for every $n$. In particular every finite family of aligned rectangular tests has its actual central law in $\widehat G$.
  - Explanation: A fixed initial window and finite geometric tables force the central law for coordinate windows of every length.
- **claim-line-slide** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-line-slide)): Let $p$ and $q$ be two chart decisions for the same oriented allowable sloping line, either primitive charts or charts in the fixed concurrent templates. Suppose a point $z$ is on this line, or within a fixed sufficiently small distance of it, and belongs to the closures of both chart boxes. On all sufficiently small rectangular cells $P$ near $z$ contained in both boxes, $p(s)$ and $q(s)$ have the same conjugation on every perfect copy controlled in $P$. The conclusion also holds when the copies are formal sloping sectors with actual rectangular control. Only the rectangular laws and the fixed geometric tables are used. At a clipping boundary the comparison is made on the common interior cells; on exterior cells the decision from that chart is zero. Thus a replacement used on both sides must retain the original clipping condition.
  - Explanation: Two chart decisions for one sloping line act identically on copies controlled in small cells near it.
- **claim-slope-rectangle-law** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-slope-rectangle)): A translated primitive sloping predicate has a joint central law with every finite aligned rectangular partition. At this stage one may retain both formal sloping sides over each rectangular cell. Every resulting perfect copy has the actual rectangular control of that cell, and hence control in all its rectangular containers.
  - Explanation: A sloping predicate has a joint central law with any aligned rectangular partition.
- **claim-inactive-line** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-inactive-line)): Let $p$ be a translated primitive sloping predicate with clipping box $B$, and let $z$ be an ordinary point not on its sloping line $L$. For sufficiently small rectangular cells $P$ near $z$ respecting the edges of $B$, the action of $p(s)$ on any perfect copy controlled in $P$ is its actual constant-side action inside $B$, and is trivial outside $B$. The assertion holds for the formal sector copies of Lemma <a href="#lem:slope-rectangle" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:slope-rectangle">36</a>.
  - Explanation: Away from its line a sloping predicate acts as a constant on small cells and trivially outside its clipping box.
- **claim-polygon-law** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-polygon-law)): There is one finite choice of true initial relations in $\widehat G$, for sufficiently large fixed finite $m$, such that every finite family of aligned polygon tests has its actual central law. The assertion holds simultaneously on subalphabets. Its canonical representations $$\rho_{U,I}:\mathop{\mathrm{Alt}}(I)^\ast\longrightarrow\widehat G$$ are compatible with restriction of the alphabet, split exactly over finite disjoint polygon refinements, and are covariant under common translations on proper supports.
  - Explanation: One finite choice of true relations gives the central law for all finite families of aligned polygon tests.
- **claim-slot-frames** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-slot-frames)): For distinct-track configurations, the representations <a href="#eq:distinct-slot-copy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:distinct-slot-copy">[eq:distinct-slot-copy]</a> have the following properties.* 1. *Replacing $U$ by $U+v$ and every $u_j$ by $u_j-v$ leaves the representation unchanged, with the corresponding reparameterization.* 2. *If $U=\bigsqcup_{\alpha=1}^r U_\alpha$, then $$\rho_S(s)=\prod_{\alpha=1}^r\rho_{S\|U_\alpha}(s), \label{eq:slot-refinement-distinct}$$ and the factor images commute pairwise.* 3. *For a constant even track permutation $c$, conjugation by its fixed lift sends $\rho_S$ to the representation of the tuple obtained by replacing $i_j$ by $c(i_j)$.* 4. *For every $d\in B_m$, $$t(d)\rho_S(s)t(d)^{-1}=\rho_{dS}(s), \qquad dS=(U;(i_j,u_j+d_{i_j})_{j=1}^5). \label{eq:distinct-translation}$$* *More generally, a representation on a subalphabet computed in an offset frame agrees with its own frame definition. Two frames giving the same offsets on that subalphabet, up to the common reparameterization in <span class="upright">(i)</span>, give identical representations there.
  - Explanation: Slot representations on distinct tracks are reparameterisation invariant, split over refinements and covariant under translations.
- **claim-fixed-slot-centralizer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-fixed-slot-centralizer)): Let $S$ be a five-slot configuration on distinct tracks, and let $b$ be an aligned word on a track set $J$. Suppose that $\pi(b)$ fixes every point of every parameterized slot of $S$. Provided the fixed track reserve contains twenty tracks outside $J$ and the occupied tracks, together with the balancing tracks used below, $b$ centralizes $\rho_S(E^\ast)$ exactly.
  - Explanation: An aligned word fixing all slot points centralises the slot representation by evacuation to private tracks.
- **claim-distinct-slot-transport** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-distinct-slot-transport)): Let $S=(U;(i_j,u_j)_{j=1}^5)$ and $T=(U;(k_j,u_j)_{j=1}^5)$ be configurations, each with distinct track indices. If an aligned word $b$ sends the $j$th parameterized slot of $S$ to the $j$th slot of $T$, then $$b\rho_S(s)b^{-1}=\rho_T(s)\qquad(s\in E^\ast), \label{eq:distinct-aligned-transport}$$ whenever the fixed reserve supplies the tracks of Lemma <a href="#lem:fixed-slot-centralizer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:fixed-slot-centralizer">40</a> for $b$ and an even constant reindexing of the two tuples.
  - Explanation: An aligned word carrying one distinct-track tuple to another conjugates the slot representations.
- **claim-routing-independence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-routing-independence)): For every five-slot configuration $S$, the homomorphism <a href="#eq:routed-copy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:routed-copy">[eq:routed-copy]</a> is independent of its admissible routing. Denote it by $\rho_S:E^\ast\to\widehat G$. It agrees with <a href="#eq:distinct-slot-copy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:distinct-slot-copy">[eq:distinct-slot-copy]</a> when the tracks are distinct. For every finite polygonal partition $U=\bigsqcup_\alpha U_\alpha$, $$\rho_S(s)=\prod_\alpha\rho_{S\|U_\alpha}(s), \label{eq:slot-refinement}$$ with pairwise commuting factor images. Common reparameterization of $U$ and the $u_j$ leaves $\rho_S$ unchanged.
  - Explanation: The slot representation on repeated tracks is independent of the admissible routing.
- **claim-aligned-slot-transport** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-aligned-slot-transport)): Let $b$ be an aligned word supported on at most five tracks. Suppose that it sends a five-slot configuration $S$ parameterwise to a configuration $T$ with constant track labels on each leg. Then $$b\rho_S(s)b^{-1}=\rho_T(s)\qquad(s\in E^\ast). \label{eq:aligned-slot-transport}$$ If track labels vary within $U$, the same assertion holds piecewise on a finite polygonal partition of $U$, with the product interpretation of <a href="#eq:slot-refinement" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:slot-refinement">[eq:slot-refinement]</a>.
  - Explanation: Small-support aligned words transport arbitrary five-slot representations by conjugation.
- **claim-translation-slot-transport** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-translation-slot-transport)): For every $d\in B_m$ and every five-slot configuration $S$, $$t(d)\rho_S(s)t(d)^{-1}=\rho_{dS}(s), \qquad s\in E^\ast, \label{eq:translation-slot-transport}$$ where $dS=(U;(i_j,u_j+d_{i_j})_{j=1}^5)$.
  - Explanation: Conjugation by a balanced translation gives the representation of the translated configuration.
- **claim-slot-generation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-slot-generation)): The subgroup $N$ is all of $\widehat G$.
  - Explanation: The slot representations generate the whole presented group.
- **claim-central-cover** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-central-cover)): For all sufficiently large fixed finite $m$, there is a finitely presented group $\widehat G$ and a surjective homomorphism $\pi:\widehat G\to A_m$ whose kernel is central.
  - Explanation: A finitely presented group surjects onto the alternating group with central kernel.
- **claim-kill-central-kernel** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-kill-central-kernel)): Let $G$ be a group with finitely generated $H_2(G;\mathbb Z)$. If there is a central extension $$1\longrightarrow K\longrightarrow E\longrightarrow G \longrightarrow1$$ with $E$ finitely presented, then $G$ is finitely presented.
  - Explanation: A finitely presented central extension of a group with finitely generated multiplier makes the group finitely presented.

        "exact_quote": "For any finite list of smooth signals constant outside compact sets, and any finite list of flag types, the laws of the independent fields in <a href=\"#eq:am-field\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:am-field\">[eq:am-field]</a> can be chosen so that, for every $g\\in\\mathcal K$, the total variation distance between their joint law and its image under $g$ is arbitrarily small on all sites at once.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-hom-finiteness-transfers",
        "exact_quote": "The following statements hold for spaces of CW homotopy type.*\n\n1.  *If $Z$ is simply connected, then, for every $r\\geq0$, $H_i(Z)$ is finitely generated for $i\\leq r+1$ if and only if $H_i(\\Omega Z)$ is finitely generated for $i\\leq r$.*\n\n2.  *Let $Y$ be a connected $H$-space. If $H_i(Y)$ is finitely generated for $i\\leq r$, then $\\pi_1(Y)$ and $H_i(\\widetilde Y)$ for $i\\leq r$ are finitely generated, provided $r\\geq1$. Conversely, if $\\pi_1(Y)$ and those cover homology groups are finitely generated, then $H_i(Y)$ is finitely generated for $i\\leq r$.",
        "source_id": "PM-253.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-hom-finiteness-transfers",
          "exact_quote": "The following statements hold for spaces of CW homotopy type.*\n\n1.  *If $Z$ is simply connected, then, for every $r\\geq0$, $H_i(Z)$ is finitely generated for $i\\leq r+1$ if and only if $H_i(\\Omega Z)$ is finitely generated for $i\\leq r$.*\n\n2.  *Let $Y$ be a connected $H$-space. If $H_i(Y)$ is finitely generated for $i\\leq r$, then $\\pi_1(Y)$ and $H_i(\\widetilde Y)$ for $i\\leq r$ are finitely generated, provided $r\\geq1$. Conversely, if $\\pi_1(Y)$ and those cover homology groups are finitely generated, then $H_i(Y)$ is finitely generated for $i\\leq r$.",
          "source_id": "PM-253.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The following statements hold for spaces of CW homotopy type.*\n\n1.  *If $Z$ is simply connected, then, for every $r\\geq0$, $H_i(Z)$ is finitely generated for $i\\leq r+1$ if and only if $H_i(\\Omega Z)$ is finitely generated for $i\\leq r$.*\n\n2.  *Let $Y$ be a connected $H$-space. If $H_i(Y)$ is finitely generated for $i\\leq r$, then $\\pi_1(Y)$ and $H_i(\\widetilde Y)$ for $i\\leq r$ are finitely generated, provided $r\\geq1$. Conversely, if $\\pi_1(Y)$ and those cover homology groups are finitely generated, then $H_i(Y)$ is finitely generated for $i\\leq r$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-perfect-covers",
        "exact_quote": "Every perfect group $E$ has a canonical perfect central extension $E^\\ast\\twoheadrightarrow E$. It is functorial in $E$. A map from $E$ into the quotient of any central extension has a unique lift from $E^\\ast$. Moreover:*\n\n1.  *two homomorphisms from a perfect group into a central extension that induce the same quotient map are equal;*\n\n2.  *if two perfect subgroups commute modulo a central subgroup, they commute exactly;*\n\n3.  *a perfect group $H$ has centerless quotient $H/Z(H)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-253.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

          "source_id": "PM-253.1"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-law-enlargement",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an alphabet $I$ with $|I|\\ge5$. Suppose a finite test family $\\mathcal T_0$ is contained in a finite family $\\mathcal T_1$, with literally the same specified input copies on the old tests, including the constant copy. Suppose they have central laws for assignment sets $\\Omega_0$ and $\\Omega_1$, respectively, and restriction of assignments gives a map $r:\\Omega_1\\to\\Omega_0$. Then their canonical representations satisfy $$\\rho^{\\,0}_{\\omega,I}(s)\n =\\prod_{\\substack{\\omega'\\in\\Omega_1\\\\r(\\omega')=\\omega}}\n       \\rho^{\\,1}_{\\omega',I}(s),\n \\qquad \\omega\\in\\Omega_0,\\quad s\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Alt}}(I)^\\ast.$$ The factors on the right commute. An old assignment with no extension has trivial original representation.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-small-support",
        "exact_quote": "There is an absolute integer $q$ with the following property. Suppose conditional perfect copies are specified coherently on the subalphabets of a finite track set, with a common assignment model $\\Omega$. If the central law holds on every alphabet of at most $q$ tracks, then it holds on the entire track set. It suffices to take $q=15$ for this implication. Additional tracks used to prove its hypotheses are a separate fixed reserve. The conclusion concerns the subgroup generated by the indicated test family, not translations or other predicates outside that family.",
        "source_id": "PM-253.1"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-253.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-translated-copies",
        "exact_quote": "Let $U$ be a basic test and let $I$ be a proper alphabet. For $u\\in\\Gamma$, choose $d\\in B_m$ with $d_i=u$ for $i\\in I$. Then $$\\label{eq:translated-copy}\n \\rho_{U+u,I}(s)\n   =t(d)\\rho_{U,I}(s)t(d)^{-1}$$ is independent of the balancing values off $I$. The same assertion holds for words and canonical perfect factors obtained from a lawful family on $I$. It has the following consequences.*\n\n1.  *Conditional copies on disjoint small alphabets commute exactly, even when their tests have different translation offsets. Products of such translated input copies inherit this property.*\n\n2.  *Translating all tests in a fixed finite table by a common $u$ preserves its exact law on a proper alphabet. Thus finite relations for bounded offsets give laws for those offsets relative to any common shift.*\n\n3.  *A translation whose coordinates vanish on $I$ commutes with all such aligned words and perfect factors on $I$. Coordinate predicates are unchanged by a shift in the other coordinate.",
        "source_id": "PM-253.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-translated-copies",
          "exact_quote": "Let $U$ be a basic test and let $I$ be a proper alphabet. For $u\\in\\Gamma$, choose $d\\in B_m$ with $d_i=u$ for $i\\in I$. Then $$\\label{eq:translated-copy}\n \\rho_{U+u,I}(s)\n   =t(d)\\rho_{U,I}(s)t(d)^{-1}$$ is independent of the balancing values off $I$. The same assertion holds for words and canonical perfect factors obtained from a lawful family on $I$. It has the following consequences.*\n\n1.  *Conditional copies on disjoint small alphabets commute exactly, even when their tests have different translation offsets. Products of such translated input copies inherit this property.*\n\n2.  *Translating all tests in a fixed finite table by a common $u$ preserves its exact law on a proper alphabet. Thus finite relations for bounded offsets give laws for those offsets relative to any common shift.*\n\n3.  *A translation whose coordinates vanish on $I$ commutes with all such aligned words and perfect factors on $I$. Coordinate predicates are unchanged by a shift in the other coordinate.",
          "source_id": "PM-253.1"
        }

The same assertion holds for words and canonical perfect factors obtained from a lawful family on $I$. It has the following consequences.*\n\n1.  *Conditional copies on disjoint small alphabets commute exactly, even when their tests have different translation offsets. Products of such translated input copies inherit this property.*\n\n2.  *Translating all tests in a fixed finite table by a common $u$ preserves its exact law on a proper alphabet. Thus finite relations for bounded offsets give laws for those offsets relative to any common shift.*\n\n3.  *A translation whose coordinates vanish on $I$ commutes with all such aligned words and perfect factors on $I$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-control",
        "exact_quote": "Assume the relevant small-alphabet laws and the exact disjoint-alphabet commutation of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:translated-copies\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:translated-copies\">28</a>. Assume also that each individual comparison leaves a fresh five-track bank and two parity-correction letters outside the alphabets involved, and one further track for balancing translations.*\n\n1.  *If the allowed assignments of a central law imply $P\\subseteq J$, they give control inside $J$; disjointness in that assignment model gives commuting perfect representations. In particular, geometric inclusion and disjointness suffice in an actual central law.*\n\n2.  *If a fragment is controlled inside $J$ and an aligned word $u$ centralizes the conditional perfect copies on $J$ on the enlarged alphabets used below, then $u$ centralizes that fragment.*\n\n3.  *Control is transitive. More generally, if two aligned words have the same conjugation action on the conditional copies on $J$, they have the same action on every fragment controlled inside $J$.*\n\n4.  *If $J,K$ have a joint central law and a fragment is controlled inside each, it is controlled inside $J\\cap K$. Fragments controlled on disjoint sides of a lawful decomposition commute, even if those fragments do not yet have a joint law with one another.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-pair-laws",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose a finite collection of partitions has a central law for each individual partition and each pair, coherently on subalphabets, and conditional copies on disjoint alphabets commute exactly.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-patching",
        "exact_quote": "In the preceding situation, with the spare tracks of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:control\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:control\">29</a> available for each individual comparison, the groups $H_P$ commute for distinct cells, generate a subgroup containing $H$, and are preserved by every original conditional generator. To prove that a word $w$ in those generators is central in $H$, it suffices to prove that it acts trivially on each $H_P$. The following verification is sufficient: for every letter of $w$ choose a replacement whose conjugation action on $H_P$ is the same; require that the replacement word acts trivially on $H_P$.*\n\n*Equality of the actions can be established by a chain of container comparisons from Lemma <a href=\"#lem:control\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:control\">29</a>. In particular, a relator central in a lawful template group acts trivially on $H_P$ if the template contains a controlling container $J$ for $H_P$ and its central law is available on the enlarged alphabets needed for the conditional move. This remains true when the individual $U_j$-sides over $P$ are only formal.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-window-bridge",
        "exact_quote": "Assume $\\mathcal R(n)$. Fix $A,K,\\delta>0$ and an integer $B\\ge1$. Suppose a perfect copy is controlled in a rectangle $R=I_x\\times I_y$ whose side lengths are at most $A/n$. Each $I_\\alpha$ is a union of cells of $\\mathcal D(W_\\alpha)$, where $$\\lfloor n/4\\rfloor\\le |W_\\alpha|\\le\\lfloor0.9n\\rfloor,$$ and the source control is available with a label margin at most $B$. Let $J=J_x\\times J_y$ be a chart rectangle whose coordinate endpoint labels each lie in an interval of at most $B$ integers. Suppose that these labels are at distance at most $Kn+B$ from a label of the corresponding source window, and that $J$ contains the closed $\\delta$-neighborhood of $R$ in the chosen circular coordinate charts. For all sufficiently large $n$, depending only on $A,K,\\delta,B$, the copy is controlled in $J$. The proof uses only $\\mathcal R(n)$.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $E$ be a perfect group, and suppose $f,l_i,h_i:E\\to H$ are homomorphisms satisfying $f(s)=l_i(s)h_i(s)$ and $[l_i(E),h_i(E)]=1$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-line-slide",
        "exact_quote": "Let $p$ and $q$ be two chart decisions for the same oriented allowable sloping line, either primitive charts or charts in the fixed concurrent templates. Suppose a point $z$ is on this line, or within a fixed sufficiently small distance of it, and belongs to the closures of both chart boxes. On all sufficiently small rectangular cells $P$ near $z$ contained in both boxes, $p(s)$ and $q(s)$ have the same conjugation on every perfect copy controlled in $P$. The conclusion also holds when the copies are formal sloping sectors with actual rectangular control. Only the rectangular laws and the fixed geometric tables are used. At a clipping boundary the comparison is made on the common interior cells; on exterior cells the decision from that chart is zero. Thus a replacement used on both sides must retain the original clipping condition.",

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-inactive-line",
          "exact_quote": "Let $p$ be a translated primitive sloping predicate with clipping box $B$, and let $z$ be an ordinary point not on its sloping line $L$.           "source_id": "PM-253.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-polygon-law",
        "exact_quote": "There is one finite choice of true initial relations in $\\widehat G$, for sufficiently large fixed finite $m$, such that every finite family of aligned polygon tests has its actual central law. The assertion holds simultaneously on subalphabets. Its canonical representations $$\\rho_{U,I}:\\mathop{\\mathrm{Alt}}(I)^\\ast\\longrightarrow\\widehat G$$ are compatible with restriction of the alphabet, split exactly over finite disjoint polygon refinements, and are covariant under common translations on proper supports.",
        "source_id": "PM-253.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-slot-frames",
        "exact_quote": "For distinct-track configurations, the representations <a href=\"#eq:distinct-slot-copy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:distinct-slot-copy\">[eq:distinct-slot-copy]</a> have the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Replacing $U$ by $U+v$ and every $u_j$ by $u_j-v$ leaves the representation unchanged, with the corresponding reparameterization.*\n\n2.  *If $U=\\bigsqcup_{\\alpha=1}^r U_\\alpha$, then $$\\rho_S(s)=\\prod_{\\alpha=1}^r\\rho_{S|U_\\alpha}(s),\n     \\label{eq:slot-refinement-distinct}$$ and the factor images commute pairwise.*\n\n3.  *For a constant even track permutation $c$, conjugation by its fixed lift sends $\\rho_S$ to the representation of the tuple obtained by replacing $i_j$ by $c(i_j)$.*\n\n\n4.  *For every $d\\in B_m$, $$t(d)\\rho_S(s)t(d)^{-1}=\\rho_{dS}(s),\n     \\qquad\n     dS=(U;(i_j,u_j+d_{i_j})_{j=1}^5).\n     \\label{eq:distinct-translation}$$*\n\n*More generally, a representation on a subalphabet computed in an offset frame agrees with its own frame definition. Two frames giving the same offsets on that subalphabet, up to the common reparameterization in <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span>, give identical representations there.",
        "source_id": "PM-253.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-slot-frames",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **simple and finitely presented group** ([source](#sec:introduction)): It is *simple* if its only normal subgroups are the trivial group and $G$, and *finitely presented* if $G\cong F(S)/\langle\!\langle R\rangle\!\rangle$ for finite sets of generators $S$ and relator words $R$.
- **lambda** ([source](#pm-h-6)): Choose a positive integer $a$, to be fixed below, and put $\lambda=\tau^a$.
- **allowable line** ([source](#pm-h-6)): An *allowable line* is $\ell_i^{-1}(t)$ with $t\in\mathcal O$.
- **Boolean polygons** ([source](#pm-h-7)): We call its elements *Boolean polygons*; they include finite unions and complements of ordinary polygonal pieces.
- **tracks** ([source](#pm-h-8)): For a positive integer $m$, write $mX=\{1,\ldots,m\}\times X$ and call its copies of $X$ *tracks*.
- **slot** ([source](#pm-h-8)): A *slot with parameter set $U$* is an embedding $e:U\longrightarrow mX$ given by finitely many translations and track changes.
- **alternating subgroup A_m** ([source](#pm-h-8)): The subgroup $A_m\le F_m$ is generated by these elements for $\sigma\in\mathop{\mathrm{Alt}}(r)$, over all finite disjoint slot collections.
- **shape** ([source](#pm-h-15)): Two nonempty polygonal cells have the same *shape* if one is a translate of the other by an element of $\mathcal O^2$ in ordinary square coordinates; their tracks may differ.
- **configuration complex** ([source](#pm-h-17)): Let $\mathcal E_m$ be the semisimplicial set whose $p$-simplices are ordered tuples $(e_0,\ldots,e_p)$ of embeddings with pairwise disjoint images.
- **polygon groupoid B** ([source](#pm-h-18)): Define $\mathcal B$ to be the groupoid whose objects are finite lists $(U_1,\ldots,U_k)$ of Boolean polygonal subsets of $X$.
- **balanced translations** ([source](#sec:lifting)): Its elements are the balanced translations.
- **central law** ([source](#pm-h-24)): We say they have the *central law for $\Omega$* if every word that evaluates to $1$ in $C_{\Omega,I}$ belongs to $Z(H)$.
- **admissible routing** ([source](#pm-h-36)): An *admissible routing* of $S$ is an aligned word $r$ on an alphabet of at most $25$ tracks containing all source and terminal track labels, whose projected action sends its five slots parameterwise to a tuple $T$ on distinct tracks.

| q-circular-separation | circular distance between distinct points i tau mod one | ≥ | $c/n$ | absolute constant c |
| q-circular-gap | largest circular gap of the points i tau mod one | ≤ | $C/n$ | absolute constant C |
| q-lambda-large | product of lambda and c | > | $1000(C+1)$ | fixed once |
| q-extension-area | ten times the area of U versus area of the complement of B | < | $10\mu(U)<\mu(mX\setminus B)$ | U, V disjoint from B |
| q-balanced-rank | rank of the free abelian group of balanced translations | = | $2(m-1)$ | positive integer m |
| q-small-support | small-support alphabet size sufficing for the central law | = | $15$ | absolute integer q |
| q-routing-tracks | alphabet size of an admissible routing | ≤ | $25$ | any configuration |
| q-window-transfers | number of window transfers in the bridge | ≤ | $\lceil20(K+2)\rceil$ | n large depending on A, K, delta, B |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-arithmetic** [claim](#stmt-lem-arithmetic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-arithmetic): Units and norms in the quadratic ring; lattice covering by unit rescaling -> bounded denominators, short tangent generators, gap and separation bounds
- **step-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-comparison): Count grid squares meeting U against whole grid squares inside V -> piecewise translation embedding
- **step-extension** [claim](#stmt-lem-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-extension): Product of two three-cycles through spare copies -> alternating element extending the bijection
- **step-simplicity** [claim](#stmt-thm-simplicity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-simplicity): Small-support representatives and double commutator -> A_m infinite, perfect, simple, equal to commutator subgroup
- **step-am-estimates** [claim](#stmt-lem-am-estimates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-am-estimates): Positive averaging matrices, covariance and Hoeffding concentration -> covariance, concentration and signal bounds
- **step-density-speed** [claim](#stmt-lem-am-density-speed) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-am-density-speed): Differentiate the density and use orthogonality of summands -> dimension-free speed bound
- **step-field-covariance** [claim](#stmt-prop-am-field-covariance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-am-field-covariance): Interpolate matrices and pass from Hellinger distance to total variation -> almost invariant bit fields
- **step-partition-coupling** [claim](#stmt-lem-am-partition-coupling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-am-partition-coupling): Maximal coupling of bit fields and endpoint transport -> covariant random polygonal partition
- **step-reiter-folner** [claim](#stmt-lem-am-reiter-folner) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-am-reiter-folner): Coset projection and level-set slicing -> finite Følner sets in the subgroup
- **step-amenability** [claim](#stmt-thm-amenability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-am-reiter-folner-2): Shape matchings turn the partition law into group measures -> F_m amenable and subgroups Følner
- **step-config-acyclic** [claim](#stmt-lem-hom-configuration-acyclic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hom-configuration-acyclic): Cone chain contraction with bounded vertex count -> acyclic configuration complex in a range
- **step-full-stability** [claim](#stmt-lem-hom-full-stability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hom-full-stability): Spectral sequence of the action on the configuration complex -> homological stability in low degrees
- **step-finiteness-transfers** [claim](#stmt-lem-hom-finiteness-transfers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hom-finiteness-transfers): Serre spectral sequences of path-loop fibration and universal cover -> finite generation transfer
- **step-cofinal-completion** [claim](#stmt-lem-hom-cofinal-completion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hom-cofinal-completion): Integral group completion with cofinal object -> stable homology as loop space homology
- **step-finite-set-bars** [claim](#stmt-lem-hom-finite-set-bars) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hom-finite-set-bars): Stability for symmetric groups, delooping, Künneth -> finite generation of finite-set bars
- **step-string-resolution** [claim](#stmt-lem-hom-string-resolution) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hom-string-resolution): Position-preserving subgroupoid and commuting-grid double nerve -> zigzag of realization equivalences
- **step-corner-module** [claim](#stmt-lem-hom-corner-module) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hom-corner-module): Injective corner germ map into a finite free module over a noetherian ring -> finitely generated module of step functions
- **step-coefficient-finiteness** [claim](#stmt-lem-hom-coefficient-finiteness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hom-coefficient-finiteness): Square-grid induction and Koszul resolution -> finitely generated coefficient homology
- **step-full-finiteness** [claim](#stmt-prop-hom-full-finiteness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-hom-full-finiteness): Finite generation of bars, then two deloopings and loop-space transfer -> H1 and H2 of F_m finitely generated
- **step-alternating-surjection** [claim](#stmt-lem-hom-alternating-surjection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hom-alternating-surjection): Augmented spectral sequence for the action of A_m on the configuration complex -> surjective stabilisation on H2
- **step-trivial-action** [claim](#stmt-lem-hom-trivial-action) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hom-trivial-action): Extend restriction to a fixed bank to an alternating element -> trivial conjugation action on H2
- **step-multiplier** [claim](#stmt-thm-multiplier) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hom-trivial-action-2): Lyndon–Hochschild–Serre spectral sequence for A_m in F_m -> H2(A_m) finitely generated
- **step-lifting-generation** [claim](#stmt-lem-lifting-generation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-lifting-generation): Boolean generation by translated tests, perfection of alternating groups, routing to private tracks -> S_m generates A_m
- **step-perfect-covers** [claim](#stmt-lem-perfect-covers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-perfect-covers): Universal central extension via free presentation -> canonical central cover with unique lifts
- **step-law-enlargement** [claim](#stmt-lem-law-enlargement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-law-enlargement): Uniqueness of lifts from perfect covers -> compatibility under enlargement
- **step-small-support** [claim](#stmt-lem-small-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-small-support): Presentation of symmetric groups and Reidemeister–Schreier rewriting -> global central law from small alphabets
- **step-translated-copies** [claim](#stmt-lem-translated-copies) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-translated-copies): Conjugation by balanced translations and independence of balancing -> translated copies and disjoint commutation
- **step-control** [claim](#stmt-lem-control) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-control): Move fragments to fresh tracks and use disjoint commutation -> container control calculus
- **step-pair-laws** [claim](#stmt-lem-pair-laws) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pair-laws): Direct decompositions of centerless quotients refine one another -> simultaneous central law
- **step-patching** [claim](#stmt-lem-patching) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-patching): Invariant cell groups and container comparison -> local verification of relators
- **step-window-bridge** [claim](#stmt-lem-window-bridge) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-window-bridge): Chain of bounded windows with bounded enlargement per step -> transfer of control to distant charts
- **step-nested-cuts** [claim](#stmt-lem-nested-cuts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-nested-cuts): Algebra of commuting prefix and suffix homomorphisms -> commuting interval factors
- **step-rectangular-law** [claim](#stmt-prop-rectangular-law) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-rectangular-law): Induction on window length using a separating inclined line -> coordinate law for all windows
- **step-line-slide** [claim](#stmt-lem-line-slide) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-line-slide): Tangent walk in short basis steps with uniform margins -> same action for chart decisions of one line
- **step-slope-rectangle** [claim](#stmt-lem-slope-rectangle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-slope-rectangle): Fine grid, concurrent template replacement, patching -> joint law of one sloping predicate with rectangles
- **step-inactive-line** [claim](#stmt-lem-inactive-line) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-inactive-line): Strict quadrant on one side of the line -> constant action away from the line
- **step-polygon-law** [claim](#stmt-thm-polygon-law) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-polygon-law): Local template comparison at each point, finite subcover, patching -> central law for all polygon tests
- **step-slot-frames** [claim](#stmt-lem-slot-frames) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-slot-frames): Offset frames and independence of balancing -> frame compatibility
- **step-fixed-slot** [claim](#stmt-lem-fixed-slot-centralizer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fixed-slot-centralizer): Evacuate slots to private tracks and use disjoint commutation -> centralisation by slot-fixing words
- **step-distinct-transport** [claim](#stmt-lem-distinct-slot-transport) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-distinct-slot-transport): Even constant reindexing reduces to a slot-fixing word -> transport between distinct-track tuples
- **step-routing-independence** [claim](#stmt-lem-routing-independence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-routing-independence): Compare two routings through distinct-track transport -> routing-independent slot representation
- **step-aligned-transport** [claim](#stmt-lem-aligned-slot-transport) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-aligned-slot-transport): Conjugate through admissible routings and refine piecewise -> aligned transport for arbitrary tuples
- **step-translation-transport** [claim](#stmt-lem-translation-slot-transport) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-translation-slot-transport): Split the translation into a blockwise shift and a centralising difference -> translation covariance of slot copies
- **step-slot-generation** [claim](#stmt-lem-slot-generation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-slot-generation): Express balanced translation generators as commutators of slot copies -> slot copies generate the presented group
- **step-central-cover** [claim](#stmt-thm-central-cover) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-central-cover): Follow slots through a kernel word and refine into constant-label pieces -> central kernel of the finitely presented cover
- **step-kill-central-kernel** [claim](#stmt-lem-kill-central-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-kill-central-kernel): Five-term exact sequence shows the kernel is finitely generated; add its generators as relators -> finite presentation of the quotient
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-kill-central-kernel-2): Fix one finite m satisfying all thresholds and combine the four properties -> infinite finitely presented simple amenable group

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-simplicity-commutator**: For every positive number of tracks, the alternating subgroup is infinite, perfect and simple, and it coincides with the commutator subgroup of the full piecewise translation group.
- **inv-amenability-subgroups**: For every positive number of tracks the full piecewise translation group is amenable, and so is each of its subgroups, including the alternating one, in the finite-set Folner sense.
- **inv-multiplier-finite-generation**: The second integral homology of the alternating group is finitely generated once the number of tracks is at least a fixed threshold.
- **inv-central-cover**: For sufficiently large finite number of tracks there is a finitely presented group with a surjection onto the alternating group whose kernel is central.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-finite-quotient-methods**: Approximating the group by finite quotients or by local embeddings into finite groups to compute with it. -> An infinite simple group has no nontrivial finite quotient, so finite-quotient approximation yields nothing faithful.
- **anti-generic-z2-amenability**: Assuming that the full group of any minimal Cantor action of the plane lattice is amenable. -> Some such full groups contain a nonabelian free group, so amenability fails.
- **anti-explicit-size-bounds**: Reading off explicit numbers of tracks, generators or relators from the theorem. -> The statement is existential; no uniform bound on the sizes is provided.
- **anti-axis-parallel-only**: Replacing the polygon model by exchanges of coordinate rectangles only. -> Rectangles lack the inclined boundary directions used to propagate relations and in the amenability argument.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result is purely existential: the number of tracks is only required to be sufficiently large, the finite relator set is chosen existentially, and the paper states that its use is existential with no decision procedure needed. No finite procedure is given that outputs the group, a presentation or a bound, so there is no executable reference to test.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main theorem is a pure existence statement about a group, with no procedure or data format a program could implement. No library or module can be derived from it. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Polygon exchanges and strict area comparison live on a Stone space of Boolean polygons modulo boundary segments. They are not a discretised physical or game state space, and the comparison lemma gives only existence of piecewise translation embeddings. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite presentation is obtained existentially from a bound on relator length. No procedure is given for listing the relators, so no presentation-extraction or checking tool follows. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The amenability argument converts almost-invariant probability measures into Følner sets by a slicing integral over a countable set, with a threshold chosen by contradiction. It does not specify a finite terminating procedure with stated cost. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The generating list is a finite set of conditional permutations and balanced translations, but the paper gives no representation, normal form or word-problem solution that could back a data structure. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result provides no dataflow, fixed-point arithmetic or parallel kernel. The simple group is an abstract object over a Stone space and has no implementable circuit counterpart. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-track-count-threshold** (hypothesis): The central cover and the finite presentation are asserted only for a sufficiently large fixed finite number of tracks, and the threshold is not quantified. The result gives no statement for small numbers of tracks. Blocked use: ['Applying the finite-presentation conclusion to a specific small number of tracks', 'Reading a concrete minimal track count off the theorem']
- **limit-homological-threshold** (hypothesis): Finite generation of the second homology of the alternating group is claimed only above an existentially asserted integer threshold on the track count, not for every number of tracks. Blocked use: ['Concluding that the Schur multiplier is finitely generated for every number of tracks', 'Combining the multiplier statement with simplicity at a track count below the threshold']
- **limit-existential-relator-set** (nonconstructive): The finite set of relators is chosen among true relations of bounded length purely existentially. The paper states that no decision procedure for membership is needed, and none is supplied. Blocked use: ['Writing down or enumerating the presentation of the group', 'Building a tool that outputs generators and relators']
- **limit-no-uniform-size-bound** (unknown): The source explicitly declines to give a uniform bound on the number of squares or on the sizes of the generator and relator sets. Their finiteness is the only asserted fact. Blocked use: ['Quoting numerical bounds on the track count or presentation length', 'Using the theorem to estimate the complexity of the word problem']
- **limit-fixed-track-amenability** (formal_scope): Amenability is proved for each fixed finite number of tracks, with the number fixed during the whole argument. No limit over increasing track numbers is involved, so the result concerns a single group rather than a union. Blocked use: ['Deducing amenability of a direct limit over increasing track numbers', 'Transferring the amenability argument to an infinite track set']
- **limit-lambda-exponent-choice** (hypothesis): The slope parameter is a sufficiently large power of the golden ratio chosen once, so that its ordinary value is large and its conjugate value is small. All constants depend on this fixed choice. Blocked use: ['Replacing the slope by an arbitrary algebraic number or a small power of the golden ratio', 'Using the arithmetic bounds with constants independent of the chosen exponent']
- **limit-slicing-threshold-existence** (nonconstructive): The Følner set is obtained by contradiction from an averaging identity over level sets, so a good threshold is shown to exist but is not computed. Blocked use: ['Presenting the Reiter-to-Følner conversion as a terminating algorithm with stated cost', 'Selecting the level threshold by a prescribed rule']
- **limit-no-finite-quotients** (counterexample_scope): An infinite simple group cannot be residually finite, so finite-quotient or local-embedding approximations carry no faithful information about this group. Blocked use: ['Approximating the group by finite quotients', 'Coset enumeration or residual-finiteness based algorithms on the group']
- **limit-amenability-not-from-rank** (formal_scope): Amenability of the full group is not a consequence of the rank of the acting group. It is proved here only for the specific polygon construction. Blocked use: ['Assuming amenability for full groups of arbitrary minimal planar Cantor actions', 'Citing this paper for amenability of generic higher-rank full groups']
- **limit-inclined-directions-required** (edge_case): The group uses two inclined edge directions besides the coordinate ones, and unions of coordinate rectangles do not give such boundaries. The results do not transfer to rectangle-only exchange groups. Blocked use: ['Replacing the polygon model by axis-parallel rectangle exchanges', 'Applying the propagation of relations to coordinate-only cuts']

> ## Scope
>
> The formalized result answers the existence question affirmatively: there is one group that is infinite, finitely presented, simple, and amenable. Amenability is expressed by the Følner condition.

> The later general claims about central kernels and enlargements across families are not included.

Comparator source `SimpleAmenable.lean` (505 bytes, sha256 `ff02a2468a0567cf22ae5ac7a7f95360c8038ad79c1df0a2a100ee2b18d0beeb`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

open scoped symmDiff

namespace SimpleAmenable

noncomputable def FolnerAmenable (G : Type*) [Group G] : Prop := by
  classical
  exact ∀ K : Finset G, ∀ ε : ℝ, 0 < ε →
    ∃ D : Finset G, D.Nonempty ∧ ∀ g ∈ K,
      (((D.image (fun d => g * d)) ∆ D).card : ℝ) < ε * D.card

theorem main : ∃ (G : Type) (_ : Group G),
    Infinite G ∧ Group.IsFinitelyPresented G ∧ IsSimpleGroup G ∧ FolnerAmenable G := by
  sorry

end SimpleAmenable

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We construct an infinite finitely presented simple amenable group, giving a positive answer to the finite-presentation existence question for simple amenable groups.
